Key Assumptions Slides
Great. So most of the research we’re going to talk about today comes out of our Tokenomics model. This is an econometric model that simulates the key cost drivers of GenAI models. We represent over 70 AI accelerators within the simulation and apply over 200 inputs to describe model design and infrastructure.
We then take this data and produce analysis based around a variety of scenarios, a collection of which are shown here.
Scenarios Slide
We publish all of these assumptions alongside our research.

So getting at your first question:
Every time I ask Gemini a medium complexity question, how much does that cost Google (ideally down to the power, GPU, etc. costs) and then what is the markup most software companies are applying?
We’re not modelling Gemini, or Claude, or ChatGPT discretely. Those are proprietary, closed-weight models where the model design isn’t public knowledge. Instead we’re making assumptions around representative “optimal” models that we’re validating against open weight models. 
INFERENCE SLIDE 1
So here we have a range of model scales we’re looking at on a log axis based on our reference scenario. We’re looking at two sets of results, on the left hand side are models using frontier silicon, on the right hand side are models using a mix of newer and older silicon, and you can see just that one difference, holding all other model parameters fixed, has a massive effect on costs. Up to 5x.
INFERENCE SLIDE 2
And then on this next slide we have a range of scenarios for a 1-trillion parameter model looking at if input costs are relatively higher or lower for inference, and then if energy constraints lead to significantly higher energy prices with the orange dashed line there.
And then I don’t have the data published yet for inference, but we do of course analyze the breakdown around energy vs. infrastructure costs. The long and short of that math is for small models, almost all of your cost/token in energy because you can push quadrillions of tokens through a datacenter over its useful lifetime. That data center may cost $10 billion, but when you break that cost up between 10 quadrillion tokens, its fractional pennies. On the other hand, bigger models require significantly more overhead, you end up with a lot of inefficiency as you need multiple networked chips to hold your model’s KV cache and weights, and your relative infrastructure cost becomes more meaningful.

Pause here, any questions before we talk about Gemini specifically?
So given all of that, to come back to your question of how much does it cost Google to run that medium complexity prompt on Gemini. Well Gemini is bouncing your query between several models. There’s an orchestrator, a relatively light model, a reasoning model, etc. The cost of that token is going to be dependent on what models Gemini uses based on the query and your license. Its all pretty opaque. There isn’t going to be a single, hard number answer for that, I think even from within Alphabet. For Q2 I’m working on a project to unpack specific use cases across several model types and providing

AIの進化に追随するためには、各事業領域や部署ごとにスピード感を重視したAI導入を促進しなければならない一方、将来的には各個AIのサイロ化やガバナンスの欠如などが課題となることが予想されます。

2. システム基盤の構成イメージ
AIオーケストレーションプラットフォームの基盤は、現状では単一製品での実現は難しいかと考えており、要件を以下の通りにモジュール単位で分割しています。
モジュールごとに最適な製品がないか、リサーチを検討したいと考えています。
<モジュール別要件>
・クライアントからのリクエストを検証・フィルタリングするガードレール機能
・リクエストの目的を推測・理解してタスク計画化し、自律的に外部AIの呼び出してオーケストレーションを行う推論エンジン
・A2A、MCP等でプラットフォーム外のAIを管理・連携する機能（社内・社外を問わずコラボレーションする）

3. ヒアリングしたい内容
①モジュールごとに定義した要件について、最適な製品・ソリューションが市場に存在するか？
②AIオーケストレーションの今後の動向、発展性について（サイロ化するAIエージェントへの対策として、オーケストレーションが最適解となり得るか？）
③AIオーケストレーションをプラットフォーム化した先進的な事例はあるか？（業種や国内外は問わず、事例があれば知りたい）

Question Asked: 拝見したレポート(AI Use-Case Assessment for Design and Engineering in Manufacturing)
で挙げられている「設計故障モード影響解析へのAI活用」について、どのようにAIを活用し、どのようなレベルのシステムの実現をこの記事を書くにあたって想定されているのかお聞きしたいという内容です。

市場品質の問題はシステムとしての複雑性増大から予知が難しくなっており、また措置費用の額も非常に大きいことからいかに設計段階で未然防止が図れるかが重要なテーマとなっている。解決策のひとつとして進化著しいAI技術の活用が考えられる。例えばChatGPTに簡単なプロンプトを投げても広範に学習している成果かベテラン設計者と見まがう回答が戻ってくる。ただし生成AIには正確性や過剰な回答などの課題があり現時点で即設計者の検討そのものを置き換えるものではないと認識している。現在進めているプロジェクトではDRBFM(変化点のみに着目してFMEAを展開し効率的に品質問題の未然予防を図る手法)の自動化、正確に言うとAIの力を借りた高度化を進めようとしているがなかなか技術的なハードルが高く先に進んでいない。先日読んだ記事(AI Use-Case Assessment for Design and Engineering in Manufacturing)の中にAIユースケースのひとつとしてAIを活用したDFMEAが実現可能性低～中程度のカテゴリに挙がっている。(以下記事での実現可能性の説明：ML、予測分析、GenAI、NLPはすべて、AI支援DFMEAの実現に貢献します。現在、市販の機能はほとんどないため、市販のオプションが受け入れられない場合は、メーカーが独自の機能を開発する必要があります。データ品質は最大の課題です。不完全、不整合、または偏りのあるデータは、リスク評価の不正確さや故障モードの見逃しにつながる可能性があります。MLに必要な時間と結果への不信感が、このAIユースケースの成長を阻害する可能性があります。)ここで言及されているデータ品質の問題は理解の上で、現状の技術プラスαでどのようなレベルの予測システムができる可能性があると考えておられるのか、エキスパートの知見・意見をお聞きしたい。
Analyst Name: Brady Barnes
Discussion Summary: Key ask:
To understand global trends and gather actionable information on AI use cases for design failure modes and effects analysis to enhance the company's predictive capabilities using historical data.

Summary:
- The discussion focused on exploring AI use cases for design failure modes and effects analysis (DFMEA) in the automotive industry.
- The customer inquired about challenges faced in quality management, indicating plans to address issues incrementally using traditional concepts like DRBFM.
- They expressed intent to leverage past design and development data to enhance predictive capabilities beyond human experience.
- The customer indicated plans to utilize historical quality issue data and documents related to new technologies like FTA and MTA.
- They are building AI-enabled prediction capabilities using past documentation, though the initiative is still in its early stages.
- The Gartner expert noted that practical applications of AI in this space are limited, with some companies in early pilot stages.
- The expert offered to share academic papers and information on commercially available solutions for comparison.
- The customer expressed interest in understanding global trends and requested resources to stay updated on AI advancements.
- The expert recommended using Gartner's Ask tool and peer connect tool to access new research and industry insights.
- The customer plans to focus on traditional mechanical data initially, with future expansion to software domains.
- They acknowledged the need for extensive, high-quality data for effective AI solutions.
- The customer is considering testing AI solutions by comparing AI-based assessments with existing failure mode data.
- The expert suggested exploring vendor demos and methodologies to evaluate the feasibility of available tools.
- The conversation concluded with the customer planning to reorganize questions and possibly follow up for further insights.

Purpose: Code Quality Review Using SonarQube
Question Asked: Hi, please can you help review "Code Quality Review using SonarQube" procedure doc
Analyst Name: Nitish Tyagi
Discussion Summary: Key ask:
To establish clear, organization-wide coding standards and code review policies, including differentiated code quality versus security criteria, for application development teams using SonarQube.

Summary:
- Vijayan sought feedback from Nitish on a document detailing code review processes, primarily focusing on SonarQube integration.
- Nitish confirmed the document’s clarity but noted SonarQube’s current focus on code quality, not security, despite acquisitions.
- Vijayan asked for resources to differentiate code quality from security in documentation for application development teams.
- Nitish advised including coding standards, quality gates, and clear policies to guide developers on SonarQube’s use and limitations.
- Nitish highlighted the need to specify SonarQube’s rule configurations and the distinction between SonarLint and SonarQube usage.
- Nitish recommended enforcing shift-left practices, integrating SonarLint reviews before PR submission to reduce obvious issues.
- He suggested moving toward “shift-down” practices, embedding automated code quality checks within self-service platforms for developers.
- Nitish emphasized clarifying roles and maturity levels in the process, advising clear definitions for level one, two, and three users.
- Vijayan confirmed SAST tools like Checkmarx are currently outside developers’ scope, handled separately and later in the process.
- Nitish advised documenting SonarQube’s limitations in detecting vulnerabilities, focusing on code health and structure issues.
- Nitish recommended visual guides and process diagrams for developers, rather than lengthy textual instructions, to improve understanding.
- He suggested leveraging SonarQube’s commercial training materials and product management resources for developer onboarding.
- Nitish advised coding standards should be organization-wide and language-specific, not just maintained by individual development teams.
- Both agreed to follow up, with Nitish sharing research notes and a summarized feedback email for further refinement and discussion.

Key takeaways:
- Nitish Tyagi will email Vijayan, Thirugnana Sambandam a summary of feedback.  
- Nitish Tyagi will share relevant research notes on programming language standardization.  
- Vijayan, Thirugnana Sambandam will set up a follow-up call after reviewing shared materials.  
- Vijayan, Thirugnana Sambandam will share current coding standards for Nitish Tyagi’s review.  
- Nitish Tyagi will provide examples or references for SonarQube developer onboarding processes.
